The Pishacha languages, which include the
Shina-Khowaar group, occupy a position intermediate
between the Sanskritic languages of India proper and
lranian languages farther to their west. They thus possess
many features that are common to them and the
Sanskritic languages. But they also possess features
peculiar to themselves, and others in which they agree
rather with languages of the Iranian family... That
language (Kashmiri) possesses nearly all the features that
are peculiar to the Pishaca and also those in which
Pishacha agrees with Eranian. 7
